It also supplied the important four-dimensional Chern-Weil gap: for delta_sigma(g) less than 1/(1+sqrt(3)), a closed oriented four-manifold must satisfy 2 chi > 3 |tau|, so Hitchin-Thorpe-violating or equality cases cannot host arbitrarily tight plus or minus normalized Ricci pinching. The Google Books object at Sessions/S0910A01/staging/acquired/Seminar_on_Differential_Geometry_-_Google_Books_8d5bd61ddad08482/content.md confirms the Seminar on Differential Geometry volume, Annals of Mathematics Studies 102, and that the Problem Section begins on printed page 669, but it does not expose Problem 26 itself. Anderson 1989 remained unrecovered except for metadata and JSTOR DOI 10.2307/1990939; an additional literal retrieval created manifest Sessions/S0910A01/trace/knowledge_request/knowledge_request_1789035603711168985.json but still did not produce Corollary B(4). Min-Oo was successfully obtained and read from Knowledge/objects/papers/ALMOST-EINSTEIN-MANIFOLDS-OF-NEGATIVE-RICCI--c331e7060106--bcf9bda031d7/object.md, a ProjectEuclid-derived full text. Its theorem: for n>3 and Lambda>0, compact smooth metrics in J^-(n,Lambda), with r<0, scale-invariant full sectional-curvature bound r^{-2} max |K| < Lambda^2, and normalized Ricci deviation max_SM |n p/r - 1| < epsilon(n,Lambda), admit a negative Einstein metric, normalized in the statement to p=-1. The proof uses normalized Hamilton Ricci flow, h=Ric-(r(0)/n)g, an L2 decay estimate from Bianchi/Weitzenbock structure, Moser iteration with Sobolev constants controlled through Ricci lower and diameter upper information, parabolic regularity, and smooth convergence; it explicitly does not assume injectivity-radius lower bounds or sectional-curvature sign, but it does require the full curvature scale bound. I also read Kröncke's Ricci-flow stability source at Knowledge/objects/papers/Stability-of-Einstein-metrics-under-Ricci-flow--601355a78e15--1a6c632e4816/object.md. It is a local dynamical theorem near existing Einstein metrics, controlled by Yamabe maximality and Laplace spectrum; it is useful for interpreting stability but does not create Einstein existence on an arbitrary underlying manifold. The second research direction tested whether negative Ricci flexibility could directly refute the negative branch. Retrieval around Lohkamp and prescribed Ricci produced the survey RICCI CURVATURE MODULO HOMOTOPY, read at Sessions/S0910A01/staging/acquired/RICCI_CURVATURE_MODULO_HOMOTOPY_dfe934e756276e2e/content.md and a promoted duplicate Knowledge/objects/papers/RICCI-CURVATURE-MODULO-HOMOTOPY--ec0fb68c286e--9206a3ce3fba/object.md. Lohkamp's survey states that every n>=3 manifold admits complete metrics with dimension-dependent negative Ricci bounds -a(n)<Ric<-b(n), every closed n>=3 manifold admits Ric<0, localized Ric<0 deformations exist, the spaces Ric<alpha(M) and S<alpha(M) are contractible Frechet manifolds and dense in C0/Hausdorff topology, and the differential relations Ric<alpha and S<alpha satisfy an h-principle. I also read Curvature Contents at Knowledge/objects/books/Curvature-Contents-of-Geometri-Spa-es--7569f99f7259--38c0d071e0d7/object.md, where Theorem 4.1 says one can locally decrease directional Ricci values: if f(nu)<Ric(g)(nu) on an open set and f=Ric(g) outside, there is a metric equal outside with Ric(g_f)(nu)<=f(nu). This is significant flexibility, but it is one-sided and curvature-decreasing; the sources read here do not show arbitrary two-sided Ricci eigenvalue intervals, nor Ricci operator uniformly close to -Id. Prescribed Ricci retrieval and reading, especially Knowledge/objects/papers/The-prescribed-Ricci-curvature-problem-for-homogeneous--04737b96562c--f25459003a14/object.md and Sessions/S0910A01/staging/acquired/Prescribed_Ricci_curvature_near_an_Einstein_manifold_with_bounda_d474bdbbee84d313/content.md, showed that DeTurck-type results are local or special-global. DeTurck local solvability holds near a point when the target tensor is nondegenerate; compact global prescription can fail; homogeneous compact results largely concern positive or semidefinite invariant targets; Delay's 2025 theorem is local near an Einstein manifold with boundary and spectral exclusions. Thus prescribed Ricci currently does not justify setting Ric=-g on arbitrary no-Einstein manifolds. I also retrieved Aliouane-Rifford-Theilliere's scalar-curvature convex-integration source, which summarizes Lohkamp's C0 Ricci-decrease theorem but concerns scalar curvature rather than full Ricci tensor. The third line sought topological obstructions. LeBrun's 4-Manifolds without Einstein Metrics was retrieved and read at Knowledge/objects/papers/4-Manifolds-without-Einstein-Metrics--c95911daf050--cd7839b9cc3d/object.md. It proves that if X is a minimal complex algebraic surface of general type and M=X#k CPbar_2, then every metric satisfies an L2 scalar-curvature lower bound greater than 32 pi^2 c1^2(X), and applying the Einstein Gauss-Bonnet formula gives no Einstein metric for k >= (2/3)c1^2(X). When (2/3)c1^2(X) <= k < c1^2(X), these manifolds can still satisfy strict Hitchin-Thorpe, providing all-sign no-Einstein smooth four-manifolds not eliminated by the elementary strict Hitchin-Thorpe requirement. Additional retrieval found Ishida-LeBrun, Sung, Kotschick, and related four-dimensional obstruction sources, plus a MathOverflow discussion at Sessions/S0910A01/staging/acquired/Is_some_closed_manifold_of_dimension___4__expected_to_18f7a496fe33ea1a/content.md indicating, as a secondary status snapshot, that no closed smooth manifold of dimension at least five was known there to be proved to admit no Einstein metric of any sign. I separately searched for positive Ricci plus no-Einstein overlap. Reiser and Burdick sources show positive Ricci/core metrics on connected sums of projective spaces and related fibre-bundle constructions, including #_k CP^2, but the retrieval did not find a verified four-dimensional manifold with Ric>0, strict Hitchin-Thorpe, all-sign no Einstein, and a plausible route to arbitrarily tight positive Ricci pinching. Examples like #_5 CP^2 admit positive Ricci by projective-space connected-sum constructions and have no Einstein by Hitchin-Thorpe violation, but the dossier's quantitative gap rules them out as arbitrarily tight plus or minus pinching candidates. I collaborated with the session group. S0910A03 sent message #196 proposing smooth Einstein-orbifold resolutions as a possible no-Einstein near-Einstein counterexample mechanism and asking for an independent challenge. I replied as #197 with the Min-Oo analysis: negative-branch counterexamples must evade the bounded full-curvature class; Lohkamp provides sign flexibility but not known tight Ricci pinching; DeTurck/prescribed Ricci does not provide global -g prescription; the orbifold route needs unweighted operator-norm Ricci defect after normalization and an all-sign topological obstruction. S0910A02 replied #198 confirming the same Min-Oo reading and keeping Anderson/Ye open. S0910A03 then sharpened the orbifold route in #199 and #200: in Morteza-Viaclovsky-type gluing, on the ALE core the normalized defect contains lambda g_cal^{-1}o+O(t^{1/2}); transition errors tend to zero; therefore a nonzero first obstruction is incompatible with uniform pinching, and only a first-obstruction-zero but higher-obstruction-nonzero variant remains live. This exchange corrected the naive hope that any obstructed desingularization gives a tight Ricci-pinched input sequence.

### Continuation

The negative-branch four-dimensional obstruction has reached a precise algebraic-analytic interface. The normalization-invariant reduction, the chiral factorization, the TTCS identity H=(1/3)I+T(z)-(W++W-)/2, and the explicit compatible jet together establish that the remaining mathematical gap is control of W- (or equivalently the derivative-twist tensor B) on the calibrated bulk. Three research directions are now sharply defined and should be evaluated for their potential to advance the overall Yau 26 goal.

(1) **Anti-self-dual energy control.** The TTCS identity shows |W-|² ≥ (3/2)(7/12+2η)² whenever H has a negative eigenvalue of size η. The global mean of |W-|² is controlled by the Noether energy bound (∫|Rm|² ≤ 12Vol in the adopted convention). If this gives ∫|W-|² ≤ C·Vol, then the volume of the set {λ_min(H) ≤ -η} is bounded by C/(3/2·(7/12+2η)²). For η > 0 fixed, this would force the 'bad H' set to have bounded (but possibly nonvanishing) volume fraction. The question is whether a stronger estimate — perhaps from the chiral Riccati structure or the Bochner identity for a- — can make the fraction vanish as the calibrated defect → 0.

(2) **Weighted compactness/reconstruction.** The quotient carries RCD(-1,4) structure with weighted doubling, Poincaré, and heat-flow regularity. A03 identified this as the correct framework. The missing theorem: on a 3D smooth RCD(-1,4) quotient where both |W+|² and |W-|² have bounded local L² means and the W+ calibrated residual → 0, does a positive-measure set of regular points admit a fixed-scale buffered cover that reconstructs a noncollapsed 4D chart? This requires fiber homotopy survival, not just quotient regularity. The Galaz-García et al. source provides the inheritance theorem; reconstruction is the new object.

(3) **Derivative-twist countermodel.** Search for an explicit near-Einstein metric (exact or approximate) on a closed 4-manifold with calibrated W+, bounded |W-|, but no fixed-scale accessible cover on most volume. The compatible jet from the TTCS shows this is algebraically possible at a point; the question is whether a global smooth solution or a sequence on closed manifolds can realize it. Taub-NUT-AdS does not qualify (uncalibrated on accessible annuli, divergent means on core-containing balls). A promising candidate might be a doubly-warped product with a slowly varying twist profile, or a Berger-type metric on an S³ bundle with prescribed Weyl anisotropy.

The positive branch still requires A02's volume-gap conjecture (conditional noncollapse for simply connected manifolds). The toric spectral analysis (A02 message #258) offers an independent approach through the Schrödinger eigenvalue bound λ₁^Dir ≤ 2(1+δ). Both branches should be compared through the common reduced equations to identify shared obstructions or shortcuts.

For the overall research evaluation: the problem has not been solved. The accumulated mathematical structure — the calibrated profile, the Derdziński germ, the chart-accessible exclusion, the normal-cover transport, the Pontryagin diffusion, the chiral factorization, and the H-W- identity — represents substantial machinery but has not yet produced a complete proof or counterexample. The next work should either close the W- control gap (which would complete the negative branch for rank-one collapse in dimension 4) or construct a definitive countermodel (which would redirect toward counterexample construction). The mixed-equation weight correction (div(v³F)=0) and the Ric[33] sign should be fixed in the reduction script before further computation.

### Continuation

The analytic platform is now structurally complete: continuation, first-exit, curvature/defect power inequalities, K_2/Y/J_6/Q chain, Moser endpoints, and terminal recalibration are all formulated and proved at the conditional level. The last major analytic/geometric piece is the **macro-disjoint support construction**, which is a measure-theoretic and geometric problem rather than a PDE problem. Specifically:

(1) **Maximal-function good centers**: For each manifold M_i in the near-critical sequence, identify a set of good centers where the dimensionless critical curvature r⁴∫_{B_r}|Rm|² ≤ ε_Li. The maximal function M(|Rm|²)(x) = sup_r r⁻⁴∫_{B_r(x)}|Rm|² controls this. The fraction of bad centers with M > ε is bounded by C·mean/ε, giving a good set of measure ≥ (1-Cε⁻¹·mean) Vol(M).

(2) **Volume-proportional assignment**: The good centers must cover supports with Vol(P_i) ≥ αA_i. This requires a lower bound on Vol(M_i)/A_i, which comes from the normalized Ricci pinching (Ric ≈ -g gives Vol ≈ (negative Chern-Gauss-Bonnet term)/A). The O(A) topology bound from Step 30 should give this.

(3) **Disjoint coloring**: Outer buffers of distinct supports must not interact. A finite coloring of the interaction graph (bounded degree from the doubling property under Ricci lower bound) gives macro-disjoint groups.

(4) **Plateau containment**: The a=1 plateau must contain all terminal exponential geodesics of length ρ from the selected center. This requires the support to extend beyond the pullback domain.

Step 86 resolved the leading-order chirality question for the product-orbifold mixed-row desingularization through three mathematical advances, building on the Step 85 Green-response computational bridge and the Strategist's boundary-duality direction.

The first advance was identifying Ozuch's Proposition 2.10 boundary pairing as the exact mathematical framework, replacing the proxy spectral sums of Steps 84-85. Reading arXiv:2105.13193 lines 449-666 revealed that the inter-node coefficient nu_l = 3 int_{S^3/Z_2} <H^4, V_{l,2}> is a spherical boundary integral between the ALE bubble's r^{-4} multipole and the receiver kernel mode's r^{+2} Taylor coefficient. The second advance was the nu_l = 0 theorem. The product orbifold's four candidate kernel modes u_i g_j have V_{l,2} proportional to C_{11} = omega_1^+ circ omega_1^- (the shape direction g_A - g_B). Both H^4(zeta^+) involving C_{2k} and H^4(zeta^-) involving C_{k2} are orthogonal to C_{11} by tr(C_{ab}C_{cd}) = 4 delta_{ac} delta_{bd}. Therefore nu_l = 0 for all kernel modes, both chiralities, every node. This was verified computationally (workspace/scripts/step86_sector_coupling_analysis.py, data/step86_sector_coupling_analysis.json). The vanishing of nu_l means the equation P_{g_o} bar_h_j^4 = 0 distributionally, so the inter-node field is the pure Green function response.

The third and most consequential advance was the complete chirality vanishing theorem. Using the S^3 quartic moment formula, the boundary pairing factorizes through products of C_{ab} matrix entries. The chirality difference is controlled by the bilinear defect tensor D_{ij,mn} = sum_k [C_{1k,ij}C_{1k,mn} - C_{k1,ij}C_{k1,mn}]. The computation (workspace/scripts/step86_spectral_chirality_computation.py, step86_chirality_parity_identity.py, step86_bilinear_defect_spectral_sum.py) revealed: (a) the squared identity holds exactly (max deviation 0); (b) the bilinear identity fails with max deviation 2.0; (c) D has rank 4 with eigenvalues +/-4 and nonzero entries only at fully-mixed (0,1,2,3)-type index quartets (the orientation tensor). But then the critical finding: every physical product eigenmode has matched tensor/angular indices — AB modes pair (iA,jB) with (iA,jB), AA/BB modes stay within one factor — so they always lie in ker(D). The contraction of D with any physical eigenmode's tensor/angular structure is zero because repeated or same-factor indices kill the fully-mixed D entries.

This gives c_opp - c_same = 0 at leading Ozuch order for ALL node pairs. The vanishing is structural, arising from three independent mechanisms: kernel orthogonality (nu_l = 0), orientation-sector confinement of D, and product-mode index matching. Any chirality difference must arise at higher order in the Ozuch expansion.

## Step 100 — 2026-09-13T07:43:31.791536+00:00

The proof uses three mechanisms: tensor-pole parity (different-pole tensor types pair and cancel), scalar-mode parity (same-pole tensor types produce Block_D = Block_O = (1/4)(D⊗D + O⊗O), cancelled by the s2 node sign), and cross-chirality sign (R_minus(O,D) flips sign, making Block_D^{+-} = -Block_O^{+-} so cross-chirality reinforces). The geometric root is EH definite chirality: d_e R^+(H^4(ζ^+)) = 0 (Ozuch lines 307/332), with the Kähler complex structure on K^+ making D⊗D + O⊗O the modulus-squared invariant under Kähler rotation.
